Each nested canonical owner identity is recursively frozen with exact key order `schema,category,ownerKey,ownerRef,ownerHash` and schema `setfarm.internal-production-canonical-owner-identity.v1`. Source identity has category `source-run`, `ownerKey=sourceRunOwnerKeyHash`, and owner ref/hash equal the operation pair. Run identity has category `run`, `ownerKey=runId=runOwnerKeyHash`, exact ref `setfarm://runs/${encodeURIComponent(runId)}` subject to the existing strict decode/re-encode rule, and `ownerHash=hashCanonicalJson({schema:"setfarm.internal-production-workflow-run-owner.v1",runId})`. The insertion authority exposes no other key or mutation seam.

`src/db-pg.ts` therefore adds one zero-input read-only protocol-authority resolver and exactly two in-transaction ports. First, `lockInternalProductionRecoverySourceBootstrapRunInsertionFenceV1(sql:PgTransactionSql,input:Readonly<{operationRef:CanonicalRef;operationHash:string}>):Promise<InternalProductionRecoverySourceBootstrapRunInsertionAuthorityV1>` returns the exact fourteen-key authority above. It locks and validates in exact order migration-v31 → owner head → active fence/targets, exact operation and release-go, zero unrelated owners/reservations, and retains those locks in the passed transaction. Second, `bindInternalProductionRecoverySourceBootstrapRunInTransactionV1(sql:PgTransactionSql,input:Readonly<{operationRef:CanonicalRef;operationHash:string;runId:string;operationRunBindingHash:string;reciprocalRunOperationBindingHash:string}>):Promise<Readonly<{sourceRunOwnerReservationRef:string;sourceRunOwnerReservationHash:string;runOwnerReservationRef:string;runOwnerReservationHash:string}>>`, called solely by the special private persistence transaction. The private order is insertion-fence port as the transaction's first database call and before any INSERT, sequence, or time allocation → allocate database sequence/time only if absent → insert or exact-adopt run/steps/context → binding port → withhold visibility until commit acknowledgement. The first port owns and returns every derivation; the second independently rederives and revalidates the held authority plus exact stored context/steps. It rederives every input, revalidates the retained migration-v31, owner-head, active-fence, and target-row authority, then locks the run row and requires zero unrelated owners/reservations and exact context/steps/protocol, and binds both pending target reservations in the same transaction. Crossed, one-sided, prebound, malformed, or unrelated state rolls back run row, steps, events, and both bindings.

Binding hashes are exact, in shown order: `operationRunBindingHash=hashCanonicalJson({schema:"setfarm.internal-production-recovery-source-bootstrap-operation-run-binding.v1",operationRef,operationHash,targetRunLaunchCompositeHash,sourceRunReservationRef:targetSourceRunReservationRef,sourceRunReservationHash:targetSourceRunReservationHash,sourceRunOwnerRef:operationRef,sourceRunOwnerHash:operationHash,runReservationRef:targetRunReservationRef,runReservationHash:targetRunReservationHash,runId,runOwnerRef,runOwnerHash})`; `reciprocalRunOperationBindingHash=hashCanonicalJson({schema:"setfarm.internal-production-recovery-source-bootstrap-run-operation-binding.v1",runId,runOwnerRef,runOwnerHash,runReservationRef:targetRunReservationRef,runReservationHash:targetRunReservationHash,operationRef,operationHash,sourceRunOwnerRef:operationRef,sourceRunOwnerHash:operationHash,sourceRunReservationRef:targetSourceRunReservationRef,sourceRunReservationHash:targetSourceRunReservationHash,targetRunLaunchCompositeHash,operationRunBindingHash})`.

`runs.task` is exactly `sourceTask` and `notify_url` is exactly null. The stored run context is canonical JSON with exact thirty-one-key order `schema,task,purpose,repository,workflow,protocol,promptManifestHash,baseSourceSha,baseSourceTreeHash,buildHash,activationPreflightHash,releaseAdmissionHash,pendingInputRef,pendingInputHash,startIntentRef,startIntentHash,startOutboxRef,startOutboxHash,operationRef,operationHash,targetSourceRunReservationRef,targetSourceRunReservationHash,targetRunReservationRef,targetRunReservationHash,targetRunLaunchCompositeHash,sourceRunOwnerRef,sourceRunOwnerHash,runOwnerRef,runOwnerHash,operationRunBindingHash,reciprocalRunOperationBindingHash` and schema `setfarm.internal-production-recovery-source-bootstrap-run-context.v1`; `task=sourceTask`. Bundled `feature-dev` must be the exact current source/build, `workflowId` is `feature-dev`, and absent or empty `workflow.context` contributes nothing—no context merge is permitted. Protocol is exactly `{mode:"v3",version:1,compilerReleaseSha:baseSourceSha,activationPreflightHash,releaseAdmissionHash,releaseAdmissionKind:"release_go",canaryAdmission:null}`.

The bundled workflow step set is nonempty, uniquely keyed, and persists in exact workflow order. Each persisted step has exact ordered keys `{id,stepId,agentId,stepIndex,inputTemplate,expects,status,maxRetries,type,loopConfig}`. `id=hashCanonicalJson({schema:"setfarm.internal-production-recovery-source-bootstrap-step-id.v1",runId,stepId:step.id,stepIndex:index})`; `stepId=step.id`; `agentId` is `feature-dev_${step.agent}`; `stepIndex=index`; `inputTemplate=step.input`; `expects=step.expects`; `status` is `pending` only for index zero and otherwise `waiting`; `maxRetries=step.max_retries??step.on_fail?.max_retries??2`; `type=step.type??"single"`; `loopConfig` is canonical JSON stringification of `step.loop` or null. Adoption and binding rederive and require the exact task, notify value, context, protocol, workflow, and every step byte.

Outbox consumption is the single database commit: no filesystem dispatch marker is added. Deterministic primary key plus exact operation/context and both binding rows make acknowledgement loss reopen/adopt; precommit crash rolls back and acknowledgement loss reopens the committed row without notification. The special dispatcher emits no refresh, event, or notification. Polling while the fence remains active must produce zero claim, execution-attempt, runtime, process, listener, or worktree byte. Existing polling after release is sufficient; its database claim CAS remains the sole maximum-one physical-dispatch authority and becomes eligible only after exact pair-close, release, source-bootstrap receipt, and terminal visibility.

The existing exact fifteen-key `InternalProductionRecoverySourceRunTerminalAuthorityV1` and exact thirteen-key `InternalProductionRecoveryRunLaunchTerminalAuthorityV1` are launch-reservation terminal evidence, not workflow-run terminal-state evidence. Receipt publishes and reopens them immediately after the acknowledged reciprocal running-row/binding commit. The source terminal's nested pair is derived exactly as `terminalOwnerHash=hashCanonicalJson({schema:"setfarm.internal-production-recovery-source-run-terminal-owner.v1",operationRef,operationHash,runId,operationRunBindingHash,reciprocalRunOperationBindingHash})` and `terminalOwnerRef=setfarm://internal-production/recovery-source-run-terminal-owner/sha256/<terminalOwnerHash>`. The run terminal's nested pair is derived exactly as `runReservationTerminalOwnerHash=hashCanonicalJson({schema:"setfarm.internal-production-recovery-run-launch-terminal-owner.v1",operationRef,operationHash,runId,runOwnerRef,runOwnerHash,operationRunBindingHash,reciprocalRunOperationBindingHash})` and `runReservationTerminalOwnerRef=setfarm://internal-production/recovery-run-launch-terminal-owner/sha256/<runReservationTerminalOwnerHash>`; the run owner pair is the insertion authority's exact nested run identity. Both bodies mean only that the fenced launch reservation completed through the exact committed reciprocal binding. They never assert a terminal `runs.status`, Tasks 1–2 completion, or global zero.

The source-run-launch target family remains exactly the same two reservations and admits no dynamic descendant target or exclusion. Immediately after both launch terminals, the existing exact-eighteen pair close atomically closes both reservations under its already frozen two-close/one-final-head-CAS while preserving the active fence; pair-close publication and the existing release CAS then clear the fence/target columns. The closed run target retains its canonical run identity and history, while the committed `runs.status='running'` row remains a live ordinary canonical `run` owner. After release there is no exclusion: ordinary census observes `activeRunCount=1`, complete-zero refuses, and claim/execution-attempt/runtime/process/worktree owners begin only through their normal lifecycles.

Spawner's existing bound-run resolver, recovery locator, `handleStepPending`, and terminal path retain ordinary `a-runtime` behavior byte-identically and add only discriminant `a-recovery-source-bootstrap-run-v1`. The special branch requires the exact stored operation pair/context, reciprocal binding, closed launch-target pair, fence release, source-bootstrap receipt, and terminal visibility deep proof; an active fence or missing release produces zero claim. Polling reads the stored operation pair and invokes the same resolver, never treating `runId` as authority. Missing/crossed/extra proof rejects. Normal no-operation payloads remain `a-runtime` only; there is no generic allow-array.

`src/execution/run-terminal-transition.ts` keeps ordinary `a-runtime` terminal resolution plus ordinary reservation close unchanged. For exact `a-recovery-source-bootstrap-run-v1`, the narrow db-pg terminal resolver deep-authenticates stored context, operation, the already closed launch-target pair, fence release, source-bootstrap receipt, reciprocal bindings, and actual run terminal authority. The existing transaction updates or exact-adopts the actual run terminal row and event but performs no ordinary reservation close, no second target close, and no owner-head transition because the special launch target was already closed before dispatch. Replay adopts exactly; crossed context, binding, launch close, release, receipt, or actual terminal rolls back. Complete-zero becomes possible only after the actual run and every descendant terminalizes through its ordinary lifecycle.

RED proves the zero-input protocol resolver's exact nine keys/literals/order, no ref/hash, unique immutable release-go selection, strict row/payload/admission-hash equality, empty slots/null expiry, stored passing preflight, deep result/gate artifacts, and read-only failure on missing/nonunique/malformed/crossed/tampered evidence. It spies that the special dispatcher permits only the named read-only protocol-authority database call before persistence and skips workspace cleanup, cron/gateway work, generic run APIs, every other database call, `refreshRunContractSafe`, `emitEvent`, `pg_notify`, and every process/filesystem mutation; the insertion-fence port is the special transaction's first database call and zero run/step/sequence/time byte exists before it. Generic pre-run persistence still refuses an active fence; special dispatch rejects extra fields and crossed operation/outbox/reservation/protocol/preflight/release/task/prompt/workflow/step projections; deterministic run/step IDs and stored database time; exact task, null notify, exact thirty-one-key context, exact protocol, and exact ten-key steps; uncommitted invisibility; rollback/commit and crash before/after row, steps, each bind, commit, both launch terminals, pair close, release, receipt, and post-release poll; parallel convergence to one run/step set/two bindings; pre-release polling produces zero downstream byte; post-release polling/claim remains maximum one. Require exact launch-terminal owner domains/hashes from a committed running row without completed/failed/cancelled status, reject forged/crossed/noncommitted/terminal-status-gated evidence, and prove activeRunCount remains one with complete-zero refusal after release. Special spawner missing-proof refuses; normal path remains unchanged; special actual terminal transition creates no ordinary close, second target close, or owner-head transition, adopts exact replay, and rejects crossed context. It also retains residual exact18 corrections: closed reservation head-version is valid only for pending/bound rows as already frozen; release acknowledgement adoption accepts the unique authenticated release ancestor rather than requiring current tip; source wrappers use current-entry `operationHash` with the controller lock and current-entry resume calls the private held-lock core.

The completion-owner bootstrap guard is durable authority rather than only a live WeakMap assertion. `InternalProductionBaselineCompletionOwnerBootstrapTargetGuardReceiptV1` has schema `setfarm.internal-production-baseline-completion-owner-bootstrap-target-guard-receipt.v1` and exact ordered keys `schema,kind,requestIdHash,claimIdHash,runIdentityHash,ownerGenerationHash,ownerFenced,ownerDrained,unrelatedOwnerCount,unrelatedOwnerCensusHash,targetGuardHash,targetGuardReceiptRef,targetGuardReceiptHash`; `kind` is exactly the opaque guard's existing `authenticated-completion-owner-bootstrap-target` literal. `targetGuardHash` hashes exactly `kind,requestIdHash,claimIdHash,runIdentityHash,ownerGenerationHash,ownerFenced,ownerDrained,unrelatedOwnerCount,unrelatedOwnerCensusHash`; the receipt hash covers `schema` through `targetGuardHash` and omits only its own ref/hash. The ref is exactly `setfarm://internal-production/baseline-completion-owner-bootstrap-target-guard-receipt/sha256/<targetGuardReceiptHash>`.

`InternalProductionBaselineCompletionOwnerBootstrapTargetGuardConsumptionV1` has schema `setfarm.internal-production-baseline-completion-owner-bootstrap-target-guard-consumption.v1` and exact ordered keys `schema,targetGuardReceiptRef,targetGuardReceiptHash,targetGuardHash,operationRef,operationHash,requestIdHash,claimIdHash,runIdentityHash,ownerGenerationHash,targetGuardConsumed,consumptionRef,consumptionHash`; `targetGuardConsumed` is literal `true`, its hash omits only `consumptionRef,consumptionHash`, and its ref is exactly `setfarm://internal-production/baseline-completion-owner-bootstrap-target-guard-consumption/sha256/<consumptionHash>`. The sole root is `data/internal-production-baseline/completion-owner-bootstrap-target-guard-v1`, with `receipts/sha256/<hh>/<targetGuardReceiptHash>.json`, `consumptions/sha256/<hh>/<consumptionHash>.json`, and the exact two-key `consumed-guards/sha256/<hh>/<targetGuardReceiptHash>.json` marker `{consumptionRef,consumptionHash}`. The marker is no-replace. Files are canonical mode `0600`; every ancestor is real mode `0700`, one device, and no symlink; descriptor/path identity is revalidated before and after each fsync/no-replace publication.

The bootstrap sequence has one distinct durable root `data/internal-production-baseline/baseline-spawner-bootstrap-restart-v1`. Its immutable records are exactly `records/continuation-grants/sha256/<hh>/<continuationGrantHash>.json`, `records/outboxes/sha256/<hh>/<outboxHash>.json`, `records/operations/sha256/<hh>/<operationHash>.json`, and `records/sequences/sha256/<hh>/<sequenceHash>.json`; restart authorization, restart authority, and startup admission remain immutable nested authorities in their already frozen owning stores. Its fixed direct-derived locators are exactly `by-target-guard/sha256/<hh>/<targetGuardReceiptHash>/operation.pair.json` plus `by-operation/sha256/<hh>/<operationHash>/continuation-grant.pair.json`, `outbox-hash.json`, `restart-authorization.pair.json`, `restart-authority.pair.json`, `startup-admission.pair.json`, and `sequence.pair.json`. Their exact ordered bodies are respectively `{operationRef,operationHash}`, `{continuationGrantRef,continuationGrantHash}`, `{outboxHash}`, `{authorizationRef,authorizationHash}`, `{receiptRef,receiptHash}`, `{startupAdmissionRef,startupAdmissionHash}`, and `{sequenceRef,sequenceHash}`. Every record and locator is canonical mode `0600`, no-replace, parent-fsynced, and reopened beneath authenticated mode-`0700` real directories; none is a mutable head. The target-guard operation locator is the earlier prose's intent/header: no separate intent body or unbound header exists. The first three locators become complete with operation publication; each of the last three authority locators may publish only after its nested authority is durable, and final sequence content precedes `sequence.pair.json`. Response loss reopens the persisted fenced authorization pair, invokes restart only with that exact pair, adopts the same restart authority, then fills any missing exact locator. Recovery derives every path from the authenticated guard/operation pair, opens the one by-guard locator and six by-operation locators at most once each in their fixed order, and never traverses, scans, selects newest/mtime, or adopts an unlinked orphan.

```ts
export type InternalProductionBaselineSpawnerBootstrapRestartOutboxV1 = Readonly<{
  schema: "setfarm.internal-production-baseline-spawner-bootstrap-restart-outbox.v1";
  kind: "completion-owner-bootstrap";
  targetGuardReceiptRef: CanonicalRef;
  targetGuardReceiptHash: Sha256V1;
  targetGuardHash: Sha256V1;
  operationId: Sha256V1;
  continuationGrantRef: CanonicalRef;
  continuationGrantHash: Sha256V1;
  preparedRuntimeSourceProjectionHash: Sha256V1;
  outboxHash: Sha256V1;
}>;
```

`outboxHash` hashes the preceding exact nine ordered fields and omits only itself. `preparedRuntimeSourceProjectionHash` is captured before operation publication; the fenced authorization copies it byte-for-byte and never recomputes it from post-restart runtime. The existing operation hash omits only `operationRef,operationHash`; the continuation-grant hash omits only `continuationGrantRef,continuationGrantHash`; the terminal sequence hash omits only `sequenceRef,sequenceHash`. Their exact ref domains are `setfarm://internal-production/baseline-spawner-bootstrap-restart-operation/sha256/<operationHash>`, `setfarm://internal-production/baseline-spawner-bootstrap-continuation-grant/sha256/<continuationGrantHash>`, and `setfarm://internal-production/baseline-spawner-bootstrap-restart-sequence/sha256/<sequenceHash>`. The continuation grant pair, prepared runtime projection, and outbox hash are prederived before the operation hash, so the operation's `continuationGrantRef,continuationGrantHash,outboxHash` commitments are acyclic. Publication order is exact: continuation-grant content → outbox content → operation content → by-operation continuation-grant/outbox locators → target-guard operation locator → completion-request operation bind. All five artifacts before the target-guard locator must be durable/reopened before prepare returns. Finalization publishes sequence content before the by-operation sequence locator. A second operation for one guard, crossed causal member, partial locator set, or unequal existing byte fails closed; a fresh-process retry adopts only the same exact pairs. The completion database result remains solely the frozen owner-lifecycle state machine: filesystem locators, not the database result, are restart-progress authority, and `guard_consumed → owner_recovered` may span authorization, restart-authority, and startup-admission locator publication only by reopening them in that fixed order.

The bootstrap wrapper's `operationId` is exactly `hashCanonicalJson({schema:"setfarm.internal-production-baseline-spawner-bootstrap-restart-operation-id.v1",kind:"completion-owner-bootstrap",targetGuardReceiptRef,targetGuardReceiptHash,targetGuardHash,postSettlementContinuationKind:"setfarm-bootstrap-main-claim-allocation-v1",bootstrapSetfarmSha,bootstrapTreeHash})` in that order. The continuation grant, bootstrap-sequence outbox, and operation each carry that byte-identical `operationId`; no caller, environment, clock, random value, later runtime, or reordered projection may derive it.

This bootstrap-sequence outbox is a predispatch durable continuation intent and is not the normal service-restart launch outbox. It never authorizes or records helper/process dispatch. Target-guard consumption must still be durable after the completion-request operation bind and before the fenced authorization or any `InternalProductionBaselineServiceRestartLaunchOutboxV1`, Task 11 helper journal, child, or physical-dispatch byte.

Runtime completion owns exactly these ports:

```ts
export function prepareInternalProductionBaselineCompletionOwnerBootstrapTargetGuardV1(
): Promise<InternalProductionBaselineCompletionOwnerBootstrapTargetGuardV1>;
export function authenticateInternalProductionBaselineCompletionOwnerBootstrapTargetGuardV1(
  guard: InternalProductionBaselineCompletionOwnerBootstrapTargetGuardV1,
): Promise<InternalProductionBaselineCompletionOwnerBootstrapTargetGuardReceiptV1>;
export function resolveInternalProductionBaselineCompletionOwnerBootstrapTargetGuardReceiptV1(
  pair: Readonly<{targetGuardReceiptRef: string; targetGuardReceiptHash: string}>,
): Promise<InternalProductionBaselineCompletionOwnerBootstrapTargetGuardReceiptV1>;
export function consumeInternalProductionBaselineCompletionOwnerBootstrapTargetGuardForOperationV1(
  input: Readonly<{
    targetGuardReceiptRef: string;
    targetGuardReceiptHash: string;
    operationRef: string;
    operationHash: string;
  }>,
): Promise<Readonly<{consumptionRef: string; consumptionHash: string}>>;
export function resolveInternalProductionBaselineCompletionOwnerBootstrapTargetGuardConsumptionV1(
  pair: Readonly<{consumptionRef: string; consumptionHash: string}>,
): Promise<InternalProductionBaselineCompletionOwnerBootstrapTargetGuardConsumptionV1>;
```

Prepare accepts no input, authenticates the current AsyncLocal request/claim/run/owner-generation tuple, requires that owner fenced and drained plus zero unrelated owners, publishes/adopts the durable receipt, and registers a new opaque handle in the local WeakMap. The arity-one authenticator is read-only, requires that exact WeakMap member, reopens the durable receipt, byte-compares every visible guard field to the WeakMap-bound receipt, and returns the resolved receipt. `prepareInternalProductionBaselineSpawnerBootstrapRestartV1(...)` must invoke it before its first operation body or locator byte. A copied/deserialized/foreign handle fails; after a crash, zero-input prepare/reopen first mints a new valid opaque handle for the same durable receipt, and that new handle authenticates without requiring the original process object. Consumption requires the current authenticated owner tuple, exact receipt, and an exact operation already durably bound to the request; it accepts no caller census or booleans. The existing completion result reserves `internalProductionBaselineSpawnerBootstrap` with exact ordered keys `schema,state,targetGuardReceiptRef,targetGuardReceiptHash,operationRef,operationHash,targetGuardConsumptionRef,targetGuardConsumptionHash,recoveredOwnerGenerationHash,targetOwnerReleaseReceiptHash,sequenceRef,sequenceHash`; `state` is exactly `guard_prepared | operation_bound | guard_consumed | owner_recovered | owner_released | completed`. Members to the right of the phase are null, and a nonnull member never changes or reverts. Existing request/owner row locks guard its expected-predecessor CAS and unrelated result keys remain byte-preserved. The only order is receipt publication and request guard bind → durable bootstrap operation → request operation bind → guard consumption → first restart outbox/helper/physical-dispatch byte; response/acknowledgement loss adopts only the same receipt, operation, and consumption.

Task 12 additionally freezes the sole complete-zero bypass for this fenced bootstrap path:

This amendment supersedes only the earlier single-body `InternalProductionBaselineServiceRestartAuthorizationV1` declaration: it becomes the schema-discriminated union above. `InternalProductionBaselineOrdinaryServiceRestartAuthorizationV1` preserves the earlier ordinary exact ten-key declaration byte-for-byte, including its schema, key order, hash projection, and existing authorization-store ref domain. The fenced member has exactly nineteen keys in the shown order; its body hash omits only `authorizationRef,authorizationHash`, and its ref uses that same existing authorization-store domain. The existing store, pair resolver, status observer/resolver, and restart consumer strict-parse the union and reject a cross-branch schema/key/pair. Normal prepare creates only the unchanged ordinary member.

Without widening the durable guard receipt, the fenced restart authority derives `ownerDrainedHash = hashCanonicalJson({schema:"setfarm.internal-production-baseline-completion-owner-drained-observation.v1",requestIdHash,claimIdHash,runIdentityHash,ownerGenerationHash,ownerDrained:true})` and `ownerFencedHash = hashCanonicalJson({schema:"setfarm.internal-production-baseline-completion-owner-fenced-observation.v1",requestIdHash,claimIdHash,runIdentityHash,ownerGenerationHash,ownerFenced:true})` in those exact member orders. Restart freshly resolves the receipt, recomputes both hashes only from it, requires equality with the existing fenced restart-authority fields, and rejects a crossed identity, reordered projection, changed boolean, or caller-supplied hash.

Only the completion sequence invokes the fenced prepare. It fixes service `setfarm-spawner`, reopens the exact bootstrap operation, receipt, and already durable consumption, requires the consumption to bind that operation/request/claim/run/generation, and emits only the nineteen-key fenced member. The existing restart operation, launch outbox, Task 11 helper registry, settlement, and maximum-one physical-dispatch path then run unchanged. For the fenced branch, `restartInternalProductionBaselineServiceV1(pair)` reopens and adopts that exact preauthorization consumption and must not consume it again; only the ordinary member retains its existing in-operation zero-owner-guard consumption. This ordering explicitly supersedes any generic earlier sentence that placed fenced guard consumption after operation/outbox publication. Normal `prepareInternalProductionBaselineServiceRestartV1({service})` still requires complete zero; the bridge cannot call or counterfeit its complete-zero producer and creates no alternative outbox/helper/dispatch mechanism.

`src/spawner.ts` owns these exact additional startup-admission ports while the startup module remains at exactly eleven callable exports:

```ts
export function prepareInternalProductionBaselineSpawnerStartupAdmissionV1(
  input: Readonly<{operationRef: CanonicalRef; operationHash: Sha256V1}>,
): Promise<InternalProductionBaselineSpawnerStartupAdmissionPairV1>;
export function resolveInternalProductionBaselineSpawnerStartupAdmissionForRestartOperationV1(
  input: Readonly<{operationRef: CanonicalRef; operationHash: Sha256V1}>,
): Promise<InternalProductionBaselineSpawnerStartupAdmissionPairV1>;
export function resolveInternalProductionBaselineSpawnerStartupClaimV1(
  pair: InternalProductionBaselineSpawnerStartupAdmissionPairV1,
): Promise<InternalProductionBaselineSpawnerStartupClaimV1>;
```

The publisher accepts only the exact normal restart operation pair, requires `operationId === input.operationHash`, freshly resolves that operation, its launch outbox, and its ordinary-or-fenced authorization, and reads only the code-owned spawner/A epoch authorities needed to derive the admission. A fenced bootstrap authorization copies its exact bootstrap operation pair; an ordinary authorization writes both bootstrap members null. Both modes require `restartLaunchOutboxHash` to equal the resolved normal restart launch outbox hash and derive the exact runtime-source projection, Setfarm SHA, spawner build, migration receipt, A activation, generic-full-verify flag, and before-generation authority without caller evidence. Publication order is admission content → exact `by-operation/.../startup-admission.pair.json` → initial fixed pending no-replace or expected-predecessor CAS from the exact completed admission pair; every member is durably reopened before any Task 11 helper registration, journal, or dispatch byte. `resolveInternalProductionBaselineSpawnerStartupAdmissionForRestartOperationV1` opens only that fixed by-operation locator and exact content; the claim resolver opens only the fixed claim derived from the exact admission pair and authenticates its exact nine keys, hash, PID/start/process identity, operation, and generation. Neither resolver scans or selects newest/mtime.

On a fresh process, zero-input active resolution first strictly authenticates the fixed pending admission pair and content, then derives only `operationRef = setfarm://internal-production/baseline-service-restart-operation/sha256/${admission.operationId}`, requires its strict decode/re-encode identity, reopens that exact normal restart operation and its by-operation admission locator, and requires the reopened admission pair byte-equal to the fixed pending pair. It registers the exact admission object, admission pair, and resolved operation pair in a private module WeakMap capability. Claim and waiter require that exact capability; a structural clone or deserialized admission fails, and neither accepts a caller `operationId`, operation pair, ref, or body. The waiter never reconstructs from a caller-supplied or unauthenticated `operationId`. This WeakMap requirement ends at active resolution, claim, and waiter; it is never passed, exported, or required by sequence execute/recover.

The receipt controller invokes the publisher by a function-local dynamic import after the normal restart outbox is durable and before Task 11 helper registration. Bootstrap publication order is therefore exact: bootstrap operation bind → target-guard consumption → fenced authorization → normal restart operation/outbox → startup admission → Task 11 helper registration/journal/dispatch. Existing ordinary guard/operation/outbox/helper relative order remains unchanged except for insertion of admission between durable outbox and helper registration. Missing, malformed, crossed, or unequal admission authority fails before helper mutation.

After execute/recover has durably published and reopened the bootstrap restart-authority locator, it deep-resolves that authority, its exact normal restart operation, and the fenced authorization. It requires the authorization's `bootstrapOperationRef,bootstrapOperationHash` equal the current bootstrap operation pair and the authority's `operationId` equal the normal operation hash. It then calls `resolveInternalProductionBaselineSpawnerStartupAdmissionForRestartOperationV1` with that normal operation pair and requires the returned admission's `operationId`, bootstrap operation pair, and `restartLaunchOutboxHash` equal the deep-resolved normal operation, authorization, and launch outbox. Only then does it no-replace publish/reopen returned exact `{startupAdmissionRef,startupAdmissionHash}` as the bootstrap operation's existing `by-operation/sha256/<hh>/<bootstrapOperationHash>/startup-admission.pair.json`. This proof is independent of the private spawner capability and works in either the original old-owner or replacement process. Response loss adopts only byte-identical exact-two content. Finalization only reads this existing locator and never invents, widens, or reconstructs it from another identifier.

`awaitInternalProductionBaselineSpawnerRestartAuthorityV1(...)` retains its existing signature and return type and now authenticates both the exact admission and fixed claim before waiting. For bootstrap mode it function-locally invokes or recovers the exact bootstrap sequence; for ordinary mode it resolves the exact authorization and restart receipt. It derives the restart-authority pair only from authenticated restart operation/authorization status and creates no sidecar. Its wait is bounded by one code-owned policy. In bootstrap mode only, after await returns and authenticates that same restart authority, `src/spawner.ts` calls `recoverAndReleaseInternalProductionBaselineCompletionOwnerBootstrapTargetV1` with the exact bootstrap operation pair, requires state `owner_released`, calls `finalizeInternalProductionBaselineSpawnerBootstrapRestartSequenceV1` with that same pair, resolves the returned sequence pair, and requires lifecycle state `completed` before entering any startup gate or producer loop. Ordinary mode calls neither function. Crash or reentry repeats and exact-adopts this chain. Zero-input active-admission resolution returns null for ordinary mode only after the exact restart authority is terminal, and for bootstrap mode only after the exact sequence and database lifecycle are completed; any partial, crossed, or unauthenticated relation remains active or fails closed.

Runtime completion additionally owns this exact eighteen-key canonical observation and three pair-only orchestration ports:

```ts
export type InternalProductionBaselineCompletionOwnerBootstrapLifecycleObservationV1 = Readonly<{
  schema: "setfarm.internal-production-baseline-completion-owner-bootstrap-lifecycle-observation.v1";
  state: "guard_consumed" | "owner_recovered" | "owner_released" | "completed";
  targetGuardReceiptRef: CanonicalRef;
  targetGuardReceiptHash: Sha256V1;
  operationRef: CanonicalRef;
  operationHash: Sha256V1;
  targetGuardConsumptionRef: CanonicalRef;
  targetGuardConsumptionHash: Sha256V1;
  startupAdmissionRef: CanonicalRef;
  startupAdmissionHash: Sha256V1;
  startupClaimHash: Sha256V1;
  restartAuthorityRef: CanonicalRef;
  restartAuthorityHash: Sha256V1;
  recoveredOwnerGenerationHash: Sha256V1 | null;
  targetOwnerReleaseReceiptHash: Sha256V1 | null;
  sequenceRef: CanonicalRef | null;
  sequenceHash: Sha256V1 | null;
  observationHash: Sha256V1;
}>;
export function recoverAndReleaseInternalProductionBaselineCompletionOwnerBootstrapTargetV1(
  input: Readonly<{operationRef: CanonicalRef; operationHash: Sha256V1}>,
): Promise<InternalProductionBaselineCompletionOwnerBootstrapLifecycleObservationV1>;
export function observeInternalProductionBaselineCompletionOwnerBootstrapLifecycleV1(
  input: Readonly<{operationRef: CanonicalRef; operationHash: Sha256V1}>,
): Promise<InternalProductionBaselineCompletionOwnerBootstrapLifecycleObservationV1>;
export function completeInternalProductionBaselineCompletionOwnerBootstrapForSequenceV1(
  input: Readonly<{
    operationRef: CanonicalRef;
    operationHash: Sha256V1;
    sequenceRef: CanonicalRef;
    sequenceHash: Sha256V1;
  }>,

```

The observation hash covers the preceding exact seventeen ordered keys and omits only `observationHash`. At `guard_consumed` its last four lifecycle members are null; `owner_recovered` sets only `recoveredOwnerGenerationHash`; `owner_released` additionally sets `targetOwnerReleaseReceiptHash`; `completed` additionally sets the exact sequence pair. A nonnull member never changes or reverts. `recoverAndReleaseInternalProductionBaselineCompletionOwnerBootstrapTargetV1` must return state `owner_released`; `completeInternalProductionBaselineCompletionOwnerBootstrapForSequenceV1` must return state `completed`. These ports accept no raw request, claim, run, owner, census, boolean, SQL, repository, callback, body, or resolver override.

Recovery deeply resolves the exact bootstrap operation, target-guard receipt and durable consumption, sequence authorization/authority locators, fenced restart authority, startup admission by its normal restart operation, and fixed startup claim. It finds the raw completion request internally by equality with the exact stored bootstrap operation pair inside reserved result member `internalProductionBaselineSpawnerBootstrap`, locks it `FOR UPDATE`, and requires exactly one match. Raw identifier, guard, operation, terminal claim, restart authority, before/after generation, and claim-generation-equals-after-generation relations are mandatory in every phase. At `guard_consumed` or `owner_recovered`, the request must be `processing`, its apply phase must be `effects_committed`, the exact old owner/lease facts remain bound, and the session must be drained. At `owner_released` or `completed`, the request must be `accepted`, apply phase must remain `effects_committed`, `accepted_at` must be nonnull, `lease_expires_at` must be null, the drained session must be released, the exact close must resolve, and no target owner may remain bound. Zero or multiple rows, any raw/hash/state/owner/attempt/generation crossing, processing after release, accepted before exact close, or an unrelated result mutation fails before change.

The first database transaction performs only the one-way reserved-result CAS `guard_consumed → owner_recovered`, sets the exact recovered generation hash, preserves every unrelated result key, and never reassigns `owner_instance`. The second requires the authenticated recovered state, releases the drained session, accepts the completion request, derives the exact completion terminal authority, closes the exact target owner reservation once, resolves that close, and atomically CASes `owner_recovered → owner_released` while setting `targetOwnerReleaseReceiptHash` to that close hash and preserving unrelated result keys. A retry already at `owner_released` bypasses mutation and adopts only the exact accepted/released/unbound/close facts above; `completed` additionally requires the exact sequence pair and resolved sequence. Partial release, a second close, a changed result, or terminal state lacking its phase authority is corruption. Fresh global zero is observed only after the release transaction is durably acknowledged.

Sequence finalization reads the exact startup-admission locator, resolves a matching `owner_released` lifecycle observation, then freshly proves global zero, publishes/reopens the terminal bootstrap sequence and exact-two sequence locator, and invokes runtime completion. Runtime completion resolves that exact sequence and CASes `owner_released → completed` while setting its sequence pair; response loss reopens and adopts the same sequence and completed result. Zero-input active admission may return null only after both completed database state and sequence authority authenticate.

Replacement execute/recover derives the expected consumption pair from the authenticated guard and bootstrap operation and resolves/adopts that durable consumption before any restart, helper, claim, or replacement effect. It invokes the capability-based consumer only when the fixed consumption record/index is absent in the initial old-owner process. A replacement process never remints or reconsumes the guard. Durable-consumption recovery requires no AsyncLocal context; absence without the original capability fails before mutation, while a crossed receipt, operation, consumption, or index always fails.

The complete invoked-only dependency additions are receipt → spawner startup-admission publisher; spawner waiter → bootstrap-sequence execute/recover, sequence finalize/resolver, receipt resolver/restart authority, and runtime lifecycle recovery; runtime completion → bootstrap-sequence, spawner, and receipt pair-only resolvers; bootstrap sequence → spawner by-operation startup-admission resolver; and bootstrap sequence → runtime lifecycle observe/complete. All reverse edges are function-local and import-inert. No helper ABI, generic restart ABI, startup exact-eleven namespace, exact24 path set, database migration, filesystem root, CLI verb, or second dispatch seam changes.
